# Design Document: Code Graph + Local Embeddings (v2 Scanner)

## 1. Goal

Upgrade the Scanner (Module A in [specification.md](specification.md)) from pure hash-based diffing into a real **node/edge code graph** with **locally-computed embeddings** on every node and edge, so that:

- The codebase can be semantically searched ("find functions like X") without any LLM call.
- Contract generation (LLM-dependent, existing Council workflow) only ever runs on nodes/edges that actually changed.
- Everything in this document — parsing, hashing, embedding — runs with **no LLM**. LLM involvement starts only at the Contract step, which is unchanged from the existing architecture.

This reuses the existing architecture: DuckDB storage, `artifacts` (nodes) and `dependencies` (edges) tables, the `code_hash` + `processing_queue` staleness mechanism. It does not replace any of that — it adds a structural/semantic layer underneath it.

## 2. Scope

- **Node granularity: function-level only**, matching the current architecture. No class/file/module nodes in this iteration.
- **Parsing: tree-sitter**, same as Module A today — this just formalizes it into an explicit node/edge graph build step rather than a flat per-function hash check.
- **Embeddings: local model only**, computed from raw source text. No API calls, no network dependency.

## 3. Data Model

### 3.1 Node hash

A node's hash is the hash of **its own content only** — no transitive/combined hashing into neighbors. This already exists as `artifacts.code_hash`.

```
node.hash = sha256(normalized_function_source)
```

### 3.2 Edge hash

An edge's hash is derived from both endpoint node hashes plus the literal call-site text:

```
edge.hash = sha256(caller.code_hash + callee.code_hash + call_site_text)
```

`call_site_text` is the actual call expression/line(s) where the call occurs — this captures changes like different arguments or a different call target, even if neither function's body changed.

**Multiple call sites between the same pair.** `dependencies` has `PRIMARY KEY (caller_id, callee_id)` today — one row per pair, regardless of how many times A calls B. Decision: **keep that primary key unchanged.** If A calls B more than once, `call_site_text` is the concatenation of every call expression found for that pair, and `edge.hash`/`embedding` are computed over that combined text. This means no data is dropped (every call site's text is included), but granularity is per-pair, not per-occurrence — you can't address or re-embed one specific call site independently of the others on the same pair. See the Decision Log (§7) for the rationale.

**Confidence when merged call sites disagree.** Each call site resolves independently (§3.5) and can land at a different confidence tier even when they target the same callee (e.g. one is a direct `self.method()` call — `EXTRACTED` — another is a dynamic/indirect call that only resolves via `--deep-scan` fallback matching — `INFERRED`). When these merge into one row, the row takes the **highest** (most certain) confidence among them — if any single call site proves the edge is real, the edge is real, regardless of how the others resolved.

**Consequence (by construction):** whenever a node's `code_hash` changes, every edge touching that node will necessarily get a new `edge.hash`, since one of the two hash inputs changed. There is no case where a node changes but its incident edges' hashes stay the same. This means propagation is **inherently bounded to one hop** — a change never needs to "spread" past the directly incident edges, because nothing beyond that boundary ever depends on the changed node's hash.

### 3.3 Schema additions

**`artifacts` (nodes) — add:**

| Column | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `embedding` | `FLOAT[384]` | Local embedding of the function source (`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, see §5). Input text is the existing `artifacts.body` column (full source already stored today) — nothing new to extract. Recomputed whenever `code_hash` changes. |

**`dependencies` (edges) — add:**

| Column | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `call_site` | `VARCHAR` | Text of the call expression(s) for this `(caller_id, callee_id)` pair. If A calls B more than once, this is every call-site text concatenated (see §3.2 "Multiple call sites"). Used both to compute `edge_hash` and as the embedding input. |
| `edge_hash` | `VARCHAR` | `sha256(caller.code_hash + callee.code_hash + call_site)`. |
| `embedding` | `FLOAT[384]` | Local embedding of `call_site` (`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, see §5). |
| `confidence` | `VARCHAR` | How this edge was resolved: `EXTRACTED` (resolved via import map or a static type annotation — explicit in source), `INFERRED` (resolved by project-wide name match with exactly one candidate — a guess, not proven), or `AMBIGUOUS` (name match found multiple candidates; stored anyway but flagged unreliable). See §3.5. |

`PRIMARY KEY (caller_id, callee_id)` on `dependencies` is unchanged. No other tables change — `queue` and `contracts` (the actual existing table name; see §3.4 correction) stay exactly as specified today.

### 3.4 Storage location

Everything — graph structure (nodes/edges), embedding vectors, and contracts — lives in the **same single DuckDB file** (`_drspec/contracts.db`). No second database, no separate vector store. This matters because running multiple storage services for a single-user local tool is exactly the ops burden that made comparable tools (e.g. Graphify) abandon a Neo4j + Qdrant setup in favor of one local file.

- Graph structure: existing `artifacts` + `dependencies` tables (relational, as today).
- Embedding vectors: new columns on those same tables (`artifacts.embedding`, `dependencies.embedding`) — not a separate file.
- Contracts: **correction** — contracts are not a column on `artifacts`. They live in the existing separate `contracts` table (`function_id` PK/FK → `artifacts.function_id`, plus `contract_json`, `confidence_score`, `verification_script`). **Edges do not get a contract** (decided — see §6/§7): the edge's `embedding`/`call_site` already serves as the lightweight, fast-searchable abstraction of that call relationship. If deeper verification than that is ever needed for a specific call site, the consuming agent reads the actual source directly rather than relying on a pre-computed, LLM-generated edge contract.

Whether similarity search over the embedding columns gets an index (e.g. DuckDB's `vss` extension, which adds an HNSW index on an array column for fast approximate nearest-neighbor search) is a separate, later decision — it changes how the column is *queried*, not where it or anything else is *stored*. Without it, similarity search still works, just as a brute-force scan over all rows.

### 3.5 Call resolution (how a call expression becomes an edge)

**This did not exist anywhere before this design.** Verified against the codebase: `ScannedFunction` (`src/drspec/core/scanner.py:77-97`) has no field for calls made in a function body, no parser (`python_parser.py`, `javascript_parser.py`, `cpp_parser.py`) extracts call expressions, and the only existing calls to `insert_dependency()` are hand-written literal strings in tests — there is no production code path that turns a call expression into a `function_id` today. Everything below is new.

Decision: **follow Graphify's approach** — resolve what can be cheaply and reliably resolved from static information already in the source, tag confidence, and skip (never guess silently) what can't be resolved that way.

**Step 1 — plain calls, module-qualified calls, and same-file calls.** For each file, parse its import statements into a map of `local name → module/file`, and keep the fresh parse's own set of function names defined in this file. This covers three shapes, all tagged `confidence = EXTRACTED`:
- **Bare name, imported:** `charge_card(...)` — look up `charge_card` directly in the import map.
- **Module-qualified:** `mymodule.helper()` (e.g. `os.path.join(...)`, `requests.get(...)`) — syntactically this is `<name>.<attr>(...)`, the same shape as an instance method call, but it is **not** a type-inference problem: `mymodule` is itself an entry in the import map (bound by an `import mymodule` statement), so `mymodule.helper` resolves immediately via that map, exactly like the bare case.
- **Bare name, same-file:** `bar(...)` called from `foo` where `bar` is defined in the same file, with no import involved at all (nothing to import — it's already in scope). Check this file's own fresh-parse function set *before* falling back further. This is actually the most certain kind of edge there is — both functions came from the exact same parse pass of the exact same file, no cross-file reasoning needed — yet it's also the single most common call pattern in most codebases (sibling helper functions calling each other), so it has to be resolved in Step 1, not left to the opt-in Step 3 guess-by-name path.
  - **Class-membership check, resolved first (needed for C++'s implicit `this`).** In C++, `this->` is optional — a member function calling a sibling method on the same instance is usually written as a bare call (`bar();`), syntactically indistinguishable from a free-function call; it never reaches Step 2a's `obj.method(...)` pattern at all. So: if the calling function is itself a method (`is_method=True`, `parent=ClassName`), first check whether `ClassName` has a method named `bar` in this same file's parse. If yes → resolve to it directly, `confidence = EXTRACTED` (this is the implicit-`this` case, just without the explicit keyword).
  - **Ambiguity guard.** Only if that class-membership check doesn't apply (not a method, or no match on the enclosing class) does it fall back to matching *any* function in the file. If more than one same-named candidate exists in the file at that point (e.g. an unrelated class's method happens to share the name with a free function) — **don't resolve it as EXTRACTED.** That's a real, if narrower, instance of the same problem Step 3 exists for (guessing among candidates), so it gets the same treatment: skip by default, or defer to `--deep-scan`'s ambiguity handling rather than silently picking one and calling it certain.

**This check must run before Step 2.** Tree-sitter parses `mymodule.helper()` and `order.process()` into the identical generic shape (`call` wrapping an attribute access, `object` + `attribute` identifiers) — it has no notion of "this object is a module" vs "this object is a variable of unknown type." So the resolution logic must explicitly check **first**: is the base identifier (`mymodule`, `order`, ...) itself a name bound by an import statement in this file? If yes → resolved here in Step 1, done. Only if no (the base is a variable/parameter, not an imported module name) does it fall through to Step 2. Mirrors Graphify's own fix for exactly this ([graphify issue #3368](https://github.com/Graphify-Labs/graphify/issues/3368)): their extractor explicitly checks whether a call's receiver is a "package-alias receiver" first, and only attempts type-based resolution when it isn't.

**Step 2a — `self`/`this` calls (checked first, before the general case below).** `obj.method(...)` where the receiver refers to the method's own instance — e.g. `self.method()` in Python, `this.method()` in JS/TS — is **not** a type-inference problem at all: the enclosing class is already known directly from this file's own fresh parse (`ScannedFunction.parent` + `is_method`), with no annotation needed. Resolution, by language:
- **Python:** the current function is a method (`is_method=True`, has a `parent` class) and the call's receiver identifier equals **that method's own first parameter name** — whatever it's actually named in the signature, not a hardcoded `"self"` string match (convention is near-universal, but not language-enforced). If it matches, look up `method` directly on `parent` (the same class, found in this same parse). Tag `confidence = EXTRACTED`.
- **JS/TS:** the receiver is the `this` (or `super`) keyword — tree-sitter gives this a distinct node type from a regular identifier, since it's a language keyword, not a parameter — inside a method body. Same resolution: look up `method` on the enclosing class. Tag `confidence = EXTRACTED`.
- **C++, explicit:** `this->method(...)` — tree-sitter's C++ grammar likewise gives `this` a distinct node type, same as JS/TS. Same resolution: look up `method` on the enclosing class (`parent`). Tag `confidence = EXTRACTED`.
- **C++, implicit (no receiver at all):** since `this->` is optional in C++, the far more common form is a bare call with no attribute access — handled in Step 1's "class-membership check," not here, since it never has an `obj.method(...)` shape to match against in the first place.

**Known scope limit:** only methods found directly on the *same* class (`parent`) are resolved this way — not inherited methods from a base class. Resolving through an inheritance chain needs the class hierarchy, which is a separate, harder problem; left out of this design, call is skipped (no edge) if the method isn't found directly on `parent`.

**Step 2b — other instance method calls via static type annotations.** For `obj.method(...)` where Step 1's check already determined `obj` is **not** an imported module name, and Step 2a's check already determined `obj` is not `self`/`this`, resolution requires knowing `obj`'s type — which isn't knowable in general without real type inference (e.g. `order.process()` could be `Order.process` or `Invoice.process` or anything else with a `.process()` method, no way to tell from text alone). Mirroring Graphify's own fix for this (they originally dropped every such call unconditionally, then added resolution via known type information — same issue as above): when `obj`'s type **is** statically declared — a type-annotated parameter (`def handle(order: Order)`), a typed field/attribute, or a similar direct annotation — resolve the declared type name (`Order`) by checking **both** the import map **and** this file's own fresh-parse class set (same fix as Step 1's "bare name, same-file" case — a type defined in the same file, like `class Order: ...` and `def handle(order: Order)` both in one file, needs no import at all, but is exactly as statically known as an imported one). Once the class is found, look up the method on it. Tag `confidence = EXTRACTED`. When `obj`'s type is **not** statically declared anywhere reachable, **skip the call entirely — no edge is created.** Never guess a receiver type.

**Step 3 — fallback name match, for anything Steps 1–2 didn't resolve. Opt-in only, via a CLI flag.**

Decision: `drspec scan` gets a new flag (e.g. `--deep-scan`), **default off**. By default, anything Steps 1–2 can't resolve is simply **skipped — no edge** (same "never guess" rule as Step 2's unannotated method calls). With `--deep-scan` passed, Step 3 runs: search all `artifacts` project-wide for a function with the called name.
- Exactly one match → resolve to it, `confidence = INFERRED` (a guess by name only, not proven by an import or annotation).
- More than one match → create **one edge row per candidate**, each `confidence = AMBIGUOUS`. These are different `callee_id`s, so they're different `(caller_id, callee_id)` pairs — this doesn't conflict with the §3.2 "combine same-pair call sites" rule, which only applies when caller and callee are the same pair. (E.g. caller calls bare `process()`, and both `file_a.py::process` and `file_b.py::process` exist: two rows get created, `(caller, file_a.py::process, AMBIGUOUS)` and `(caller, file_b.py::process, AMBIGUOUS)`, rather than guessing one or dropping the information.)
- Zero matches (e.g. the call is to a stdlib function, a third-party package, or something genuinely external) → no edge. `dependencies` only ever tracks calls between functions that are themselves nodes in this project — matches the current architecture's scope, nothing new here.

**Why opt-in:** `INFERRED`/`AMBIGUOUS` confidence depends on the *global* set of same-named functions project-wide, not on the caller's own code. Since §4's re-scan trigger only fires on a node's own `code_hash` changing, that confidence tag can go stale without the caller ever being touched again — e.g. a second same-named function added elsewhere should flip an `INFERRED` edge to `AMBIGUOUS`, but nothing re-checks it. Making Step 3 opt-in means this risk only exists for users who explicitly asked for the extra coverage; default-mode edges (`EXTRACTED` only) are never subject to it. For `--deep-scan` users, this staleness is an accepted known limitation — a full rebuild re-runs resolution against current global state and fully corrects it.

## 4. Algorithm (CLI, no LLM)

**Decided: folded into the existing `drspec scan` command** (`src/drspec/cli/commands/scan.py`), not a new separate command. Concretely, this extends `scan.py` right after its existing `insert_artifact` call.

**Important existing-state note:** today, nothing in production actually populates `dependencies`. `insert_dependency()` exists (`src/drspec/db/queries.py`) and the table/schema exists, but `scan.py` never calls it — it's currently only exercised by tests. `drspec deps get`/`drspec deps plot` can read the table, but there's no write path feeding it. So this design isn't just adding embeddings on top of an existing graph-build step — it's also what first wires up real dependency-graph extraction in production. One command (`scan`) ends up doing the full no-LLM pipeline: parse → hash-diff → write/delete nodes → write/delete edges → embed nodes and edges.

1. Tree-sitter parse changed files, extract function nodes (as today).
2. **Node removal (file-level diff):** for each scanned file, diff the fresh parse's set of `function_id`s against the `artifacts` rows currently stored for that `file_path`. Any stored `function_id` no longer found in the fresh parse is treated as **hard-deleted**, cascading in this order (no `ON DELETE CASCADE` is defined in `schema.sql` today, so this must be done explicitly by the app, not relied on from DuckDB):
   1. `dependencies` rows where it's `caller_id` or `callee_id`
   2. `reasoning_traces`, `vision_findings` rows for that `function_id`
   3. `queue` row for that `function_id`
   4. `contracts` row for that `function_id`
   5. the `artifacts` row itself

   A rename is indistinguishable from a delete+new-function at this layer — it's treated as delete-old + insert-new, with no link preserved between the old and new `function_id`. No history of the old function's contract/reasoning is kept.
3. For each remaining (still-present) node, compute `code_hash`.
   - **Unchanged** → skip entirely. No embedding work, no edge work. This is the cheap early-exit that keeps most of a large graph untouched on a small diff.
   - **Changed or new** →
     a. Update node metadata (signature, file path) from the fresh parse.
     b. Recompute the node's embedding from raw source → write to `artifacts.embedding`.
     c. Update `artifacts.code_hash`; set `status = STALE` so the existing `processing_queue` mechanism picks it up for contract regeneration.
     d. **As callee:** fetch every existing `dependencies` row where this node is `callee_id` (its callers didn't change, but the edge hash depends on this node's hash too — see §3.2). For each: recompute `edge_hash` and `call_site`-based `embedding`.
     e. **As caller:** run **§3.5 call resolution** over this node's body to get its fresh set of (callee, call_site, confidence) — this is new logic that doesn't exist today (see §3.5). **The diff is scoped to the confidence tiers this invocation actually attempted to resolve**, not all stored rows unconditionally:
        - **Plain scan (no `--deep-scan`):** only Steps 1–2 ran, so the fresh set only ever contains `EXTRACTED` calls. Diff this fresh set against this caller's **existing `EXTRACTED` rows** for delete/update purposes: delete missing `EXTRACTED`, update common `EXTRACTED`. Existing `INFERRED`/`AMBIGUOUS` rows for this caller are never deleted or downgraded by a plain scan — this invocation never attempted Step 3 and has no basis to say those calls stopped existing.
        - **`--deep-scan` run:** Steps 1–3 all ran, so the fresh set is complete. Diff for delete/update purposes against **all** of this caller's existing rows regardless of confidence — delete missing, update common, across every tier.

        **Insert/promotion check (applies to both modes, since `PRIMARY KEY (caller_id, callee_id)` holds exactly one row per pair regardless of confidence):** before inserting any callee the diff considers "new," check whether `(caller_id, callee_id)` already exists **at any confidence tier**, not just the tier this invocation is scoped to.
        - No existing row at any tier → genuine **insert**.
        - A row already exists at a *different*, lower tier (e.g. a plain scan's Step 1 now resolves a pair that's currently stored as `AMBIGUOUS`/`INFERRED` from an earlier `--deep-scan`) → this is a **promotion**, not an insert: **update** that row's `confidence` to `EXTRACTED` (and its `call_site`/`edge_hash`/`embedding`), not a new row. A plain scan *is* allowed to do this even though it otherwise can't touch `INFERRED`/`AMBIGUOUS` rows — promotion only ever moves a row toward more certainty, never claims a call stopped existing, so it doesn't carry the false-deletion risk the "leave untouched" rule above exists to prevent.

        Without this scoping and promotion check, a plain scan run after an earlier `--deep-scan` run could either silently delete a still-valid `INFERRED`/`AMBIGUOUS` edge (if the scoping fix above were missing), or attempt to insert a row that collides with an existing lower-confidence row under the same primary key (if this promotion check were missing) — both silent-incorrectness paths, not real reflections of what changed in the code.
4. Stop. Nothing beyond the directly incident edges is touched — no queue, no multi-hop walk, no risk of runaway cascades.

## 5. Embedding Model

**Decided: `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, run via `fastembed` (Qdrant's ONNX runtime), not `sentence-transformers`.** ~22M params, **`N = 384`** output dimension (fixes the `FLOAT[384]` columns in §3.3). `fastembed` was picked over `sentence-transformers` specifically to avoid a PyTorch dependency — this session hit a heavy, unexpected source rebuild just installing DuckDB, which made clear that pulling in PyTorch (a far bigger, slower, more failure-prone install) for an embedding step this small isn't worth it. `fastembed` runs CPU-only via ONNX Runtime, no GPU/torch dependency at all. Weights are downloaded/installed once locally; no network call happens during actual scan execution (to be verified for real in Task 2.10, not just assumed).

Known trade-off, accepted: this is a general-purpose text model, not code-specialized. It embeds source code as if it were prose, which will likely give noticeably weaker semantic-search quality than a code-specific model (e.g. `jina-embeddings-v2-base-code`) would. Chosen anyway for its smaller size/speed and because it's the more common/simpler default. Revisit if semantic search quality on real code turns out to be insufficient in practice.

**Embedding input:**
- Node: `artifacts.body` (the function's full source, already stored) — not signature-only, not a summary.
- Edge: `dependencies.call_site` (the call-site text, combined across multiple call sites if applicable — see §3.2).

## 6. Open Questions

None remaining — all resolved, see Decision Log (§7).

## 7. Decision Log

| Date | Decision | Rationale |
|------|----------|-----------|
| 2026-10-27 | Node hash = hash of own content only, no transitive/combined hashing | Keeps hashing simple and avoids a graph-structure dependency just to compute a hash; see §3.1 |
| 2026-10-27 | Edge hash = `sha256(caller.code_hash + callee.code_hash + call_site)`, one-hop propagation only | By construction, any node change already changes every incident edge's hash — no multi-hop walk is needed or beneficial; see §3.2 |
| 2026-10-27 | Embedding input is raw source (`artifacts.body` for nodes, `call_site` for edges), not a signature/summary | Simplest pipeline, no LLM needed anywhere in this scan step |
| 2026-10-27 | Function-level nodes only (no class/file/module nodes) | Matches current architecture; broader granularity deferred until needed |
| 2026-10-27 | Edges do not get their own LLM-generated contract | The edge's `embedding`/`call_site` already serves as a fast, searchable abstraction of the call relationship; a full call-site precondition/postcondition contract adds detail that isn't needed when the agent can just read the source directly for anything deeper |
| 2026-10-27 | Keep `dependencies` PRIMARY KEY (caller_id, callee_id) unchanged; combine multiple call-site texts into one row rather than one row per call site | No current consumer (BFS traversal, cycle detection, CLI) needs per-call-site granularity; matches Graphify's own default behavior (they only split via an opt-in multigraph mode); since edges don't get contracts either (see below), there's no feature that would need per-call-site precision |
| 2026-10-27 | All storage (graph, embeddings, contracts) stays in the single existing DuckDB file; no second database or vector store | Avoids the multi-service ops burden that led comparable tools (e.g. Graphify) to abandon a Neo4j + Qdrant setup |
| 2026-10-27 | On every scan of a changed caller, full set-diff its callees against stored `dependencies` rows — insert new, delete missing, update common | Keeps the graph exactly matching current code every scan; avoids stale edges accumulating when calls are removed (rejected insert-only: it lets `dependencies` silently drift from reality with no cleanup path) |
| 2026-10-27 | A `function_id` missing from a file's fresh parse is hard-deleted, with explicit app-level cascade to `dependencies`/`reasoning_traces`/`vision_findings`/`queue`/`contracts` (no `ON DELETE CASCADE` in schema) | Consistent with the edge full-set-diff decision above; rejected soft-delete (`status = REMOVED`) because it accumulates orphaned data with no automatic cleanup. Known cost: a rename is indistinguishable from delete+new, so no history links the old and new `function_id` |
| 2026-10-27 | Embedding model: `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, `N = 384` | General-purpose, small (~22M params), CPU-only, common default in `sentence-transformers`/`fastembed`. Known accepted trade-off: not code-specialized, so semantic search quality on source code will likely be weaker than a code-specific model like `jina-embeddings-v2-base-code`; revisit if that proves insufficient |
| 2026-10-27 | Fold graph+embedding extraction into the existing `drspec scan` command rather than a new separate command | `scan.py` already fuses parsing/hashing/diffing for nodes; extending the same command keeps the whole no-LLM pipeline as one step. Also the point where dependency-graph writing (`insert_dependency`, currently dead code outside tests) finally gets wired into production |
| 2026-10-27 | Call resolution (§3.5): import-map for plain calls, static-type-annotation walk for method calls, fallback project-wide name match otherwise; tag every edge `EXTRACTED`/`INFERRED`/`AMBIGUOUS`; never guess a receiver type with no static annotation | Mirrors Graphify's real-world approach, including its own documented history of initially dropping all method calls unconditionally (see [graphify#3368](https://github.com/Graphify-Labs/graphify/issues/3368)) before adding type-annotation-based resolution. Confirmed this logic doesn't exist anywhere in drspec today — this is new, not an extension of something partially built |
| 2026-10-27 | Ambiguous name-match resolution creates one edge row per candidate (each tagged `AMBIGUOUS`), not a single guessed row or a dropped edge | Each candidate is a different `callee_id`, so they're naturally different `(caller_id, callee_id)` pairs — doesn't conflict with the combined-call-site-text decision, which only applies within one pair |
| 2026-10-27 | Fallback name-match resolution (§3.5 Step 3) is opt-in via a new `drspec scan --deep-scan` flag, default off | `INFERRED`/`AMBIGUOUS` confidence depends on the global set of same-named functions, not the caller's own code, so it can go stale without the caller ever being re-scanned (§4 only triggers on a node's own `code_hash` changing). Making it opt-in keeps default-mode edges (`EXTRACTED` only) immune to this entirely; `--deep-scan` users accept the staleness as a known limitation, correctable by a full rebuild. Rejected building targeted incremental invalidation for it — extra schema/trigger-path complexity for a problem the project's existing rebuild story already solves |
| 2026-10-27 | Module-qualified calls (`mymodule.helper()`) are checked against the import map in Step 1 *before* Step 2's type-inference path runs, not treated as an instance-method call by default | Tree-sitter parses `mymodule.helper()` and `order.process()` into the identical `<name>.<attr>(...)` shape with no semantic distinction; without an explicit "is the base name an imported module?" check running first, this extremely common pattern (`os.path.join`, `requests.get`) would be wrongly routed into Step 2 and silently dropped. Mirrors Graphify's explicit "package-alias receiver" check ([graphify#3368](https://github.com/Graphify-Labs/graphify/issues/3368)) |
| 2026-10-27 | Same-file bare calls (`bar()` called from `foo` in the same file, no import involved) are resolved in Step 1 against this file's own fresh-parse function set, tagged `EXTRACTED` | Oversight caught via review: as originally written, same-file calls matched none of Steps 1–2 (no import statement, not an attribute call) and would have silently fallen through to the opt-in, guess-by-name Step 3 — or produced no edge at all by default. This is likely the single most common call pattern in most codebases, and also the most certain (both functions come from the same parse pass of the same file), so it belongs in Step 1, not treated as a guess |
| 2026-10-27 | `self`/`this` method calls get a dedicated Step 2a, resolved directly against the enclosing class (`ScannedFunction.parent`) via `is_method` + matching the method's own first-parameter name (Python) or the `this`/`super` keyword node (JS/TS) — not via Step 2b's type-annotation path | Oversight caught via review: as originally written, `self.method()`/`this.method()` has no type annotation to walk (it's the implicit receiver, not an annotated parameter), so by the letter of the original Step 2 every such call would be skipped by default — likely the single most common method-call pattern in any OOP-heavy codebase, including this project's own (e.g. `Scanner` calling its own methods). Deliberately checks the method's *actual* first-parameter name rather than hardcoding the string `"self"`, since that's convention, not a language rule. Known scope limit: only resolves methods found directly on the same class, not inherited ones — inheritance-chain resolution is left out |
| 2026-10-27 | Added explicit C++ handling: `this->method()` resolved in Step 2a (same keyword-node approach as JS/TS `this`); bare implicit-`this` calls (`bar();`, no receiver at all since `this->` is optional in C++) resolved in Step 1 via a class-membership check (same enclosing class checked first), falling back to plain same-file matching only if no class-member match is found | Oversight caught via review: C++ is a declared first-class language (`schema.sql`'s `language` column lists python/javascript/cpp), but the original Step 2a only covered Python/JS patterns. C++'s optional `this->` means the common case has no `obj.method(...)` shape at all, so it would never have reached Step 2a's pattern-match and would have been handled (wrongly, with no class restriction) by the generic same-file rule |
| 2026-10-27 | Same-file bare-call resolution (Step 1) only tags `EXTRACTED` when exactly one candidate matches in the file; more than one (e.g. an unrelated class's method collides by name with a free function) is treated like Step 3's ambiguity, not silently resolved | Without this guard, same-file resolution could pick the wrong callee and still mark it `EXTRACTED` — implying certainty the match doesn't actually have, specifically a risk the class-membership check for C++ surfaced |
| 2026-10-27 | §3.5's resolution engine (import map lookup, same-file + ambiguity guard, self/this handling, type-annotation walk, fallback match, confidence tagging) lives in one new shared module, not duplicated inside each of `PythonParser`/`JavaScriptParser`/`CppParser` | Verified the existing parsers are fully separate per-language classes with no shared base class (duck-typed only) — that's fine for "extract functions from source," but §3.5's *decision logic* needs to behave identically across languages, and three independent copies would inevitably drift (as this review already found happening within a single language's logic). Each parser keeps its existing job but gains a small new one: produce a language-agnostic fact set (import map, each call's name/receiver/declared-type) for the shared engine to consume. See §8 |
| 2026-10-27 | §4 step 3e's edge diff is scoped to the confidence tiers this scan invocation actually attempted: a plain scan only deletes/updates `EXTRACTED` rows for a caller, leaving existing `INFERRED`/`AMBIGUOUS` rows from a prior `--deep-scan` run completely untouched; only a `--deep-scan` run diffs across all tiers | Oversight caught via review: the original unconditional full-set-diff predates `--deep-scan` being made opt-in. As written, a plain scan run after an earlier `--deep-scan` would see old `INFERRED`/`AMBIGUOUS` rows as "missing from this run's fresh parse" and delete them — silent, incorrect data loss, since the call never actually changed, only the resolution mode did. Rejected "never mix modes" as the fix — an easy-to-violate documentation-only constraint, not an actual guarantee |
| 2026-10-27 | Before inserting a "new" callee, check for an existing row at *any* confidence tier for that `(caller_id, callee_id)` pair, not just the scoped tier; if one exists, UPDATE (promote) it instead of inserting. Promotion to `EXTRACTED` is allowed even from a plain scan | Oversight caught via review: the scoped-diff fix above compares "insert new" only against same-tier rows, but `PRIMARY KEY (caller_id, callee_id)` has no confidence component — a pair already stored as `AMBIGUOUS`/`INFERRED` would look "new" to a same-tier-only comparison and collide on insert. Promotion is safe to allow even in plain-scan mode (unlike deletion) because it only ever moves a row toward more certainty, never asserts a call stopped existing |
| 2026-10-27 | When call sites merged into one row (§3.2) resolve at different confidence tiers, the row takes the highest (most certain) tier | Gap caught via review: §3.2's merge rule predates the confidence column. Chosen over "take the lowest": if any single call site proves the edge is real, the edge is real — a second, weaker-resolved call site to the same callee doesn't undo that |
| 2026-10-27 | Step 2b's declared-type lookup checks this file's own fresh-parse class set, not just the import map | Oversight caught via review: a type-annotated parameter very commonly refers to a class defined in the same file (no import needed, already in scope) — same category of gap as Step 1's original same-file-call miss, just not carried over to the type-annotation path |
| 2026-10-27 | Embedding runtime: `fastembed` (ONNX Runtime), not `sentence-transformers` | §5 had left this undecided ("sentence-transformers or fastembed"). `sentence-transformers` pulls PyTorch as a dependency — a much larger, slower, more failure-prone install than this project needs for one small embedding step. Decided after this session's own DuckDB install hit an unexpected heavy source rebuild, making the cost of a large ML dependency concrete rather than theoretical |
| 2026-10-27 | Reordered remaining tasks: CLI skeleton moved up to Task 3 (with call resolution stubbed), real resolver and real per-language fact extraction pushed to Tasks 4–5 as "replace the stub" steps | Deliberate switch to a walking-skeleton/tracer-bullet approach at the user's request — get the whole `drspec scan` pipeline runnable end-to-end first (using the already-real, already-verified Task 1/2 work), proving the wiring/integration points early, then fill in the hardest piece of logic (call resolution) behind an already-proven seam, rather than building every piece in full isolation before anything is runnable |
| 2026-10-28 | Task 4 includes a crude, throwaway, Python-scoped regex fact-builder (`fact_builder.py`), not just the resolver logic tested against synthetic inputs | User's explicit choice between the two options §4.2 posed. Validated immediately: dogfooding the real resolver through this fact-builder against drspec's own source found two real bugs (masking gap, import multi-line parsing) that hand-built synthetic `ResolutionInput`s alone would never have exercised |
| 2026-10-28 | An attribute call with no declared type reachable anywhere does not fall through to Step 3's opt-in fallback, even under `--deep-scan` | §3.5's own text is ambiguous here: Step 2b says "never guess a receiver type" without qualifying it to non-deep-scan mode, while Step 3 is framed as "anything Steps 1-2 didn't resolve." Read Step 2b's "never" as the more specific, overriding rule — guessing a method by bare name alone, with no idea what class it's even on, is a materially worse guess than a bare free-function guess |
| 2026-10-28 | `call_resolver.py` masks out triple-quoted strings and `#`/`//`/`/* */` comments (preserving length/newline positions) before scanning for calls | Oversight caught via dogfooding: Task 3's "docstring literal" limitation (e.g. `>>> foo(...)` doctest examples) was assumed to be a rare corner case from one observed instance, but affected ~12% of all edges on the real codebase. Cheap enough to fix properly (a masking pass, not a full parse) rather than leave as an accepted gap. Regular single/double-quoted string literals are still left unmasked — a smaller, still-accepted remaining gap |
| 2026-10-28 | Self-signature exclusion no longer pattern-matches specific prefix text (`"def "`, or "nothing") — instead, the *first* bare occurrence of the caller's own name is always treated as its own signature, unconditionally; only later occurrences can be genuine recursion | Oversight caught via dogfooding: the scanner's stored function body boundary turned out to have a *third* variant beyond "def included" / "def excluded" — a partially-truncated case (`"f _scan_single_file("`) that neither existing check could catch. This is a latent bug in the scanner itself (out of scope to fix here), but the resolver doesn't need to know which variant it's looking at if it just trusts that the first self-named match is always the declaration |
| 2026-10-28 | `fact_builder.py`'s import parser explicitly handles multi-line parenthesized `from X import (...)` statements by locating the matching `)`, not just a same-line regex | Oversight caught via dogfooding: this exact style (used in `graph_sync.py`'s own imports) silently resolved to nothing with the original same-line-anchored regex. Common real-world import style (e.g. black/isort formatting), not a rare corner case worth leaving broken |
| 2026-10-28 | The keyword-skip branch of `resolve_calls`'s self-match guard (JS/C++-style bodies that keep the `function`/leading-type keyword) now also marks the caller's own name as "seen," matching what the no-keyword branch (Python-style bodies) already did | Correctness bug caught by user review, not by this task's own dogfooding — introduced by the prior fix for case #2 above. Without this, the first genuine bare self-recursive call in a JS/C++-style body was wrongly mistaken for the (already-skipped) signature and silently dropped — confirmed empirically both as a missing edge (one recursive call) and as silently-truncated `call_site` text (two recursive calls, first one lost). The dogfood run's only self-recursion example went through the attribute-call (`self.`) path, which was never in the buggy branch, so bare self-recursion was never actually exercised despite the dogfooding claim |
| 2026-10-28 | A bare call immediately preceded by `super()` / `super().` is skipped outright, never resolved via the Step 1 class-membership check or any other path | Bug caught by a second user review after noticing the re-dogfooded edge count (291) didn't match this document's own previously-claimed count (284) — investigating the discrepancy (rather than assuming it was explained by the keyword-skip fix above) surfaced that `super().method(...)` was parsing as a bare call to `method`, which then wrongly resolved to the *caller's own* class instead of its parent's. The keyword-skip fix had been accidentally masking this the whole time (both bugs independently suppressing the same call sites), which is also why this task's own dogfooding never surfaced it as a separate issue. Treated as the same "inheritance-chain resolution is out of scope" limit §3.5 Step 2a already states, just reached via the bare-call path |
| 2026-10-29 | Task 5's real per-language fact extraction changes `resolve_calls()`'s interface (option (b) from §5.1): `ResolutionInput` carries pre-extracted `call_sites: list[CallSite]` from real `call`/`call_expression` AST nodes, not raw `caller_body` text. `_mask_non_code`, `_CALL_PATTERN`, and the whole self-signature-exclusion/`seen_own_name` machinery are deleted, not just unused | User's explicit choice between the two options §5.1 posed. A `function_definition` node is never a `call` node, so the entire class of Task 3/4 bugs built around that confusion (docstring/comment masking, the body-boundary inconsistency, the `seen_own_name` branch bug) is structurally impossible here, not newly patched. Also removes the `queue.py::prioritize` string-literal gap, confirmed gone on re-dogfooding |
| 2026-10-29 | JS/TS declared-type extraction (§3.5 Step 2b) is not implemented, for JS/TS specifically | Found to be a hard dependency constraint while implementing Task 5.4, not a scope choice: this project only installs `tree-sitter-javascript`, which doesn't parse TypeScript type-annotation syntax at all — confirmed empirically before concluding this, not assumed. Adding real support would need `tree-sitter-typescript` as a new dependency, out of scope for this task |
| 2026-10-29 | C++'s `using namespace X;` and `#include` are not tracked as imports at all; only `using X::Y;` (a specific bound name) is | Neither `using namespace` nor `#include` binds one specific symbol the way Python/JS imports do — there's nothing for either to resolve *to* without guessing which of potentially many symbols a caller meant. Matches C++'s own language semantics (no symbol-level import binding), not an arbitrary scope cut |
| 2026-10-29 | Import resolution (`project_facts.py`'s `_functions_by_name`) excludes methods — only a file's top-level (non-method) functions are ever matched against an import or module-qualified access | Bug caught by dogfooding a real JS fixture (Task 5.11): a free function and a same-named method in the same file (free `add()` alongside a `Calculator.add` method) collided on the bare name "add", silently resolving an import to whichever the DB query happened to return last. There's no `from X import SomeClass.method` syntax in Python or JS — a method was never a valid import target to begin with |
| 2026-10-29 | `ResolutionInput` gets a new `bare_calls_imply_self` field, set `True` only for C++ callers (`graph_sync.py`); the Step 1 class-membership check (bare call implicitly means `this->method()`) only runs when it's set | Bug caught by dogfooding a real JS fixture (Task 5.11): the class-membership check was being applied to every language, but only C++ makes `this->` optional — Python and JS always require an explicit `self.`/`this.` for a method call, so a bare call inside a JS method was wrongly resolving to a same-named sibling method instead of the free function it actually meant |
| 2026-10-29 | `same_file_functions` (the general "Step 1 same-file" pool, not the C++-specific class-membership check) excludes methods entirely, in every language | Correction of Task 3/4's own stated rationale, caught while fixing the bug above: the original ambiguity-guard rationale ("an unrelated class's method happens to share the name with a free function") assumed real name-resolution semantics would consider an unrelated class's method a candidate for a bare call — they don't, in Python, JS, *or* C++ (a method on a class you're not inside is never in scope for a bare identifier in any of the three). Excluding methods here makes the resolver strictly more correct (resolves genuinely unambiguous cases it previously flagged as false ambiguity) without losing any real protection — the ambiguity guard itself is kept, since it still has genuine value for same-bare-name collisions between a top-level function and a same-named nested function elsewhere in the file |

# 8. Implementation Note: Shared Call-Resolution Module

§3.5's logic should **not** be implemented three times inside `PythonParser`/`JavaScriptParser`/`CppParser`. Those three classes stay exactly as they are today — fully separate, no shared base class, each owning its own tree-sitter grammar and AST-walking for "extract functions from this file." That part of the architecture isn't being changed.

What's new: a single shared module (e.g. `src/drspec/core/call_resolver.py`) holding the entire §3.5 decision tree — the EXTRACTED/INFERRED/AMBIGUOUS logic, the step ordering (module-qualified → same-file → self/this → type-annotation → opt-in fallback), the ambiguity guard, all of it — written once.

Each parser gains one small additional responsibility: produce a language-agnostic set of facts for the resolver to consume, per function:
- the file's import map (`local name → module/file`)
- for each call site in the body: the called name, whether the receiver is a self/this-style reference (and if so, what the receiver token actually is — e.g. the method's first parameter name in Python, or confirmation it's a `this`/`super`/C++-`this` keyword node), and any statically declared type for the receiver (parameter annotation, typed field, etc.), if present

The language-specific part is small and mechanical (spotting these facts in each language's own AST shape); the part that must stay consistent and correct (the actual resolution decisions) lives once.

  **Three real bugs found and fixed during this task, none of them hypothetical** — all caught either by the tests above or by dogfooding `drspec scan` against this project's own `src/drspec/core/` (per the standing dogfooding instruction):
  1. **Model-download progress bar leaking into CLI JSON stdout.** `fastembed`'s first-ever download prints a tqdm progress bar; depending on stream redirection this can land in the same output as `drspec scan`'s JSON. Fixed in `embedder.py` by calling `huggingface_hub.utils.disable_progress_bars()` before first model load.
  2. **Every Python function spuriously "called itself."** Discovered scanning this project's own `hasher.py`: `ScannedFunction.body` for Python excludes the `def` keyword entirely (confirmed directly — body starts at the function name itself), so the stub's keyword-based self-definition check (which looks for `def`/`function`/`class` immediately before a match) never fires for Python, and every function's own signature self-matched as a call to itself. Fixed by adding an explicit `caller_name` field to `ResolutionInput` and excluding a match only when it's both the function's own name *and* at the very start of its body (whitespace only before it) — genuine self-recursion elsewhere in the body still resolves correctly (see `test_genuine_self_recursion_still_resolves`).
  3. **DuckDB (through at least 1.2.2, the ceiling on this project's Python 3.8 floor — confirmed no 1.3.2 wheel exists for cp38) raises a spurious FK `ConstraintException` when UPDATE-ing an array-typed column (`artifacts.embedding FLOAT[384]`) on a row any other table still references** — not just a `dependencies` edge; a routine `queue` entry from the function's own first scan is enough to trigger it. A plain-column UPDATE on the same referenced row is fine; only the array column is affected. Fixed with a new `_fk_references_detached()` context manager in `queries.py` that temporarily removes every referencing row across all five tables with `REFERENCES artifacts(function_id)` (`dependencies`, `queue`, `contracts`, `reasoning_traces`, `vision_findings`), runs the UPDATE, and restores them byte-for-byte in a `finally` (so a failed UPDATE still can't silently lose rows). Covered by two new regression tests in `test_db_queries.py`.

  **One known, accepted stub limitation, documented rather than engineered around:** a call-shaped pattern inside a docstring or comment (e.g. a doctest example like `>>> compute_hash(...)` inside `compute_hash`'s own docstring) can spuriously resolve as a self-call, since the stub's regex has no notion of comments/docstrings. Found via the same `hasher.py` dogfooding run. Not fixed here — properly fixing it means duplicating `hasher.normalize_code`'s comment-stripping logic, and Task 5's real tree-sitter facts don't have this problem at all (a docstring literal is never a `call` AST node). Documented in `call_resolver.py`'s module docstring.

  Full suite: 1059 passed, same one pre-existing unrelated failure as Task 2 (`test_get_learning_stats`, DuckDB `.venv-review` version drift). Dogfooded against `src/drspec/core/` itself (17 files, 106 functions, 52 real edges resolved) — rescanning afterward is a true no-op (106 unchanged, 0 edges touched), confirming the no-op property holds at real scale, not just on fixtures.

  **Judgment call, not explicit in design.md — logged here since it resolves a real ambiguity in §3.5's own text:** an attribute call with no declared type anywhere reachable does **not** fall through to Step 3's fallback, even under `--deep-scan`. §3.5 Step 2b says "never guess a receiver type" without qualifying it to non-deep-scan mode, while Step 3 is framed as "anything Steps 1-2 didn't resolve" — read literally these two sentences conflict. Resolved by treating Step 2b's "never" as the more specific, overriding rule: guessing a method by bare name alone, with no idea what class it's even on, is a materially worse guess than a bare free-function guess, so it stays out of scope for Step 3 regardless of mode. Tested explicitly (`test_unannotated_receiver_never_guesses_even_with_deep_scan`).

  **Four real bugs found and fixed via dogfooding** (`drspec scan` against this project's own `src/drspec/`, ~452 functions, 65 files) — the first two are the Task 3 "doctest-literal" limitation turning out to have far more impact than estimated plus a second, previously-unknown root cause behind the same symptom; the third and fourth were both caught by a user review re-checking this task's own work, not by this task's own dogfooding (see why, for each, below) — **this section's own edge/self-loop counts were corrected twice as a result; see the final paragraph for the actual current numbers, not the ones quoted mid-investigation below**:
  1. **Call-shaped text inside docstrings/comments** (Task 3's documented limitation) affected ~38 of ~320 edges (~12%) on the real codebase — common doctest examples like `>>> success_response({...})` inside a function's own docstring, not the rare corner case a single example suggested. Fixed properly rather than left documented: `call_resolver.py` now masks out triple-quoted strings and `#`/`//`/`/* */` comments (preserving length and newline positions, so match offsets and call-site line extraction stay valid) before scanning for calls at all. Regular single/double-quoted string literals are still scanned as-is (lower-priority remaining gap, documented in code — see bug #4's `prioritize` case below for a real instance of this exact gap).
  2. **The scanner's stored function body boundary isn't consistently "`def` fully included" or "`def` fully excluded.`"** Found a real third case while re-dogfooding after fix #1: `_scan_single_file`'s body started with `"f _scan_single_file(\n..."` — a *partially* truncated `def` keyword, which neither the keyword-text check nor the "pure whitespace before" check could catch, since it's neither. Fixed by replacing both of those checks (for the caller's *own*-name case specifically) with a more robust invariant: the *first* bare occurrence of the caller's own name followed by `(` is always its own signature, regardless of exactly which characters got truncated around it; any *later* occurrence is genuine self-recursion and still resolves. This pre-existing scanner inconsistency is itself a latent bug in Module A (not touched here — out of scope for Task 4), but the resolver no longer needs to know or care which exact variant it's looking at.
  3. **Fix #2 silently dropped genuine bare self-recursive calls in JS/C++-style bodies** (caught by a user review, not this task's own dogfooding — see why, below). `seen_own_name` was only ever set inside the no-keyword branch (Python's body shape); the keyword-skip branch (`_preceded_by_definition_keyword`, which correctly skips the *real* signature for JS/C++-style bodies that keep the `function`/leading-type keyword) left it `False` — so the first genuine bare recursive call later in the body tripped the same-name guard and was wrongly swallowed as "the signature" instead. Confirmed empirically before fixing: `function foo(n) { ...; return foo(n - 1); }` resolved to `[]` (the only call site, entirely lost); with two recursive calls, the first was silently dropped from the merged `call_site` text while the edge itself still resolved — a *subtler* data-loss bug than a missing edge, exactly the kind that passes a casual code read. Fixed by setting `seen_own_name = True` in the keyword-skip branch too, whenever that skipped occurrence is the caller's own name. Confirmed via a real C++ fixture through the full `drspec scan` pipeline: `int factorial(int n) { ...; return n * factorial(n - 1); }` now correctly resolves `factorial -> factorial`.

     **Why this task's own dogfooding didn't catch it:** the dogfood run's only confirmed self-recursion example (`JavaScriptParser._extract_functions` calling `self._extract_functions(...)`) goes through the *attribute*-call path (`self.`), which was never in the buggy branch — bare self-recursion with no `self.`/`this.` prefix (a plain function, or a C++ method recursing without `this->`) wasn't exercised by that spot-check.

     Immediately after landing this fix, a **second user review** (checking the fix itself, not the original work) spotted that the re-dogfooded edge count (291) didn't match this document's own claimed count (284) from right before the fix — correctly refusing to guess at a cause and flagging it as an open discrepancy instead. Investigating it surfaced bug #4:
  4. **Fix #3 *unmasked* a different, pre-existing bug that it had been accidentally hiding.** `super().method(...)` (e.g. `super().__init__(...)`) has no receiver shape the regex can represent — `super()`'s closing `)` sits between `super` and the `.`, so it parses as two separate bare calls, `super(` and then `method(`. The second one hit the Step 1 class-membership check and resolved to the **caller's own** same-named method — not its parent's, which is what `super()` actually means: a genuinely wrong edge, not just a missing one, which is a sharper violation of the project's "never guess" principle than any prior gap. Before fix #3, this same bare `method(` occurrence usually got *swallowed* by the (then-buggy) same-name-signature guard instead — the two bugs were canceling out. Fixed by detecting the `super()` / `super().` prefix and skipping it outright: §3.5 Step 2a already scopes inheritance-chain resolution out of this design ("known scope limit... left out"), and this is that same limit, just reached through the bare-call path instead of the attribute-call one. Confirmed against the real codebase: all 4 `__init__`-via-`super()` false self-loops and one `super().default(...)` one disappeared after the fix, with no other edges affected.

     **Why neither this task's dogfooding nor the first re-check caught it:** bug #3's fix had, as a side effect, already been masking bug #4 for most of this project's own `super()` call sites throughout this task's earlier dogfood runs — there was never a point where dogfooding saw bug #4 in isolation to notice it, only ever the combination of both bugs looking approximately (and wrongly) like "no edge," which reads as correct ("never guess") rather than suspicious. It took a human re-verifying the edge *count* itself, not just spot-checking a few edges for plausibility, to notice anything had changed at all.

  **This episode is the clearest instance so far of a lesson worth generalizing, not just logging once:** dogfooding confirms the paths it happens to exercise, and a fix to one bug can silently uncover — or silently keep hiding — a different one occupying the same symptom. A spot-check of "does this one example now look right" is not the same guarantee as "is the aggregate count stable and does every self-loop have a traceable, confirmed-genuine cause," and this task got that distinction wrong twice before a second human pass caught it both times.

  **Current, re-verified numbers** (superseding every count quoted above, which reflected the code state *at the time that specific bug was found*, not the final state): dogfooding the *entire* `src/drspec` tree after all four fixes gives **453 functions, 65 files, 287 edges, 15 self-loops** — every one of the 15 re-confirmed as genuine self-recursion (AST walkers, recursive serializers, recursive-descent parsers) **except** `cli/commands/queue.py::prioritize`, which is bug #1's already-documented, already-accepted remaining gap (regular string literals aren't masked — the literal text `"...prioritize (format: ...)"` inside a `typer.Argument(help=...)` string matches the call pattern). Left as-is, consistent with that gap's original scope decision, rather than opening a fifth fix in the same sitting.

  New `src/drspec/core/fact_builder.py` (Task 4.2, ~180 lines): `build_same_file_functions`, `build_classes_by_name` (project-wide, queries `artifacts.parent`), `build_project_function_index` (project-wide, deep_scan only), `determine_self_aliases`, `parse_declared_types` (Python signature regex), `resolve_imports_for_file` (Python-only import parsing, resolved against already-scanned artifacts by file-stem matching — never guesses when a stem match is ambiguous). Also found and fixed during this task: the import parser initially missed multi-line parenthesized `from X import (\n    a,\n    b,\n)` statements entirely (a regex anchored to one line can't span them) — found by dogfooding this exact style in `graph_sync.py`'s own imports. Fixed with `_iter_from_import_items`, which locates the matching `)` explicitly instead of assuming single-line.

  17 new tests in `tests/unit/test_fact_builder.py` (lighter coverage than the resolver's, deliberately — this module is replaced outright by Task 5, not permanent) plus 38 total in `tests/unit/test_call_resolver.py` (was 7; +2 for bug #3, +4 for bug #4). All of Task 3/4's *intentional* behavior changes are captured in the Decision Log below, not silent.
  - [x] 5.10. Re-dogfooded the full `src/drspec` tree: 467 functions (+14 from Task 4's 453 — fully explained by new code *this task itself added* to `src/drspec`, e.g. `project_facts.py` and the new parser methods, being scanned like any other source), 483 edges (+196 from 287 — real AST extraction catching vastly more genuine calls the crude regex/import-resolution never could, confirmed correct by inspection, not assumed), 20 self-loops, **all independently re-confirmed as genuine recursion** (including this task's own new `_collect_calls`/`_extract_imports` methods, which are themselves recursive) — zero false positives, and the known `queue.py::prioritize` gap is gone, exactly as 5.1's choice of option (b) predicted.
  - [x] 5.11. Dogfooded both JS and C++ against real fixture projects (not just synthetic tests) — found two real bugs, below.

  **Two real bugs found via the real JS/C++ dogfooding this task explicitly required (5.11)** — neither was hypothetical, both are now fixed with regression tests at both the unit and CLI-integration level:
  1. **A free function and a same-named method in the same file collided when resolving an import or module-qualified access.** `_functions_by_name` (now in `project_facts.py`) built its name→function_id map from *every* artifact in a file, including methods — so a file with both a free `add()` and a `Calculator.add` method would have the import `{ add } from "./math_utils"` resolve to whichever one the DB query happened to return last, a real guess, not a resolution. Fixed by excluding methods (`a.parent is not None`) from that map — imports only ever bind a module-level name, and there's no `from X import SomeClass.method` syntax in either language, so a method was never a valid target in the first place.
  2. **The C++-specific "class-membership" check (bare call implicitly means `this->method()`) was being applied to *every* language, not just C++.** A real JS fixture (`Calculator.add` calling bare `add(this.value, x)`, with a free `add()` also in scope) exposed this: the bare call resolved to `Calculator.add` itself — wrong, since JS (like Python) always requires an explicit `this.`/`self.` for a method call; a bare name never implicitly means "my own method" outside of C++'s specifically-optional `this->`. Fixed by adding `ResolutionInput.bare_calls_imply_self` (set `True` only for C++ callers in `graph_sync.py`), gating the class-membership check on it. This also surfaced that the *general* same-file pool (`same_file_functions`) should never have included methods at all, in any language — see the Decision Log entry below for why that's a correction of Task 3/4's own stated rationale, not just a new restriction.

  Full suite: 1132 passed, same one pre-existing unrelated failure as Tasks 2-4.

  - [x] 6.1. Unit-test coverage per resolution case already satisfied by Task 5's `test_call_resolver.py` (38 tests) and `test_project_facts.py` (13 tests) — same-file, imports, self/this, declared-type, fallback, confidence-merge, and the two Task 5.11 language-gating fixes are all covered; no additional resolver unit tests needed here.
  - [x] 6.2. Integration pass over this project's own codebase, exercising the CLI commands themselves (`scan`, `deps get`, `deps plot`), not just re-scanning. Found four real issues, each independently confirmed against the actual source (not assumed):
    1. **Duplicate artifacts from inconsistent scan paths** ([scanner.py:120](src/drspec/core/scanner.py#L120) area, `scan_file`/`scan_directory`'s `rel_path` handling) — `function_id` is built from whatever literal path string reaches the scanner: relative if a `relative_to` root was given, otherwise `str(path)` verbatim, which can be absolute. Nothing normalizes this to one canonical form, so scanning the same file via two different invocation styles (e.g. an absolute path vs. a relative one) creates two disconnected `function_id`s for the same real function. Confirmed empirically: a second scan of an already-scanned file via a different path style took artifact count from 467 to 471, not 467.
    2. **CLI flags silently fail after the path argument** ([scan.py:16](src/drspec/cli/commands/scan.py#L16), the `scan` sub-`Typer`) — `drspec scan <path> --flag` fails with a misleading "No such command '--flag'" error, giving no hint it's an ordering issue; only `drspec scan --flag <path>` works. Pre-existing Click/Typer nested-sub-app quirk, not new to `--deep-scan` — also affects `--no-queue`.
    3. **`deps plot` labels truncated to near-unreadability** ([grapher.py:296](src/drspec/visualization/grapher.py#L296)) — hardcoded 12-char + `"..."` cutoff with no override option; most real function names in this codebase (`build_classes_by_name`, `_resolve_fallback`, `sync_scan_results`, ...) render illegibly in the generated graph.
    4. **`--deep-scan` has no effect on an already-scanned, unchanged codebase** ([graph_sync.py:150](src/drspec/core/graph_sync.py#L150)) — the per-function "unchanged → skip" early exit runs before any confidence-tier logic and determines which functions even reach edge resolution at all, regardless of `--deep-scan`. Technically consistent with the documented unchanged-skip rule (§4 step 3), but means there's no way to say "go back and try harder on what's already unresolved" short of a full rebuild.
  - Also observed, but not formally confirmed as a finding: a small (481 vs. 483) edge-count drift between two otherwise-identical dogfood scans of the same codebase, with a similar 1-self-loop difference. No specific line or cause was identified — flagged here narratively rather than claimed as a confirmed bug.

User reviewed the four findings and asked for #1, #2, and #3 to be fixed; #4 was judged a documented limitation consistent with this project's existing "rebuild rather than patch incremental staleness" philosophy (§7's `--deep-scan` opt-in decision), not a bug to engineer around — left as-is.
    1. **Fixed** — `function_id`/`file_path` is now always computed relative to `Path.cwd()` (the project root, where `_drspec/` lives — the same anchor the DB path itself already uses), for both single-file and directory scans, regardless of how the scanned path was spelled on the command line. `Scanner.scan_directory`/`iter_scan_directory` gained an optional `relative_to` parameter (defaults to the scanned directory, preserving prior library-level behavior for callers that don't pass it) which `cli/commands/scan.py` now always passes as `Path.cwd()`. Verified: scanning a file via a directory scan, a relative single-file path, and an absolute single-file path all now produce the exact same `function_id`, with the second and third scans reporting `functions_unchanged` rather than `functions_new`.
    2. **Fixed** — `scan` is no longer mounted as a nested `Typer` sub-app (a Click `Group`, which stops accepting its own options once it sees the first positional argument — a real Click/Typer limitation, not a drspec-specific bug). It's now registered directly as a command on the main app (`app.command(name="scan")(scan.scan_command)` in `cli/app.py`), which is a plain Click `Command` and accepts flags in either order. Verified both `drspec scan <path> --deep-scan` and `drspec scan --deep-scan <path>` now work identically; `--help` output unchanged.
    3. **Fixed** — removed the hardcoded 12-char truncation in `grapher.py`'s label-drawing step; labels now render the full function name, with a small semi-transparent background box added so text stays legible over edges/other nodes at the smaller font size now needed. Verified by dogfooding `deps plot` against a real long name in this codebase (`build_classes_by_name`), which now renders in full.
    4. **Left as documented limitation, not fixed** — `--deep-scan`'s no-retroactive-effect on an unchanged codebase is consistent with the project's existing accepted trade-off that `INFERRED`/`AMBIGUOUS` resolution can go stale and is correctable by a full rebuild (§7, "Fallback name-match resolution... is opt-in"), not a new engineering gap worth a special-cased fix.

- [x] 9. Investigate and fix §11's `drspec scan` performance issue — see §11 for the original write-up; this task corrects its central hypothesis (confirmed by verification §11 itself asked for, not assumed) and implements the fix that's actually verified to work.
  - [x] 9.1. **§11's proposed fix direction (batching into fewer `execute()`/`executemany()` calls) does not work — verified empirically, not assumed.** Benchmarked individual `execute()` calls vs. `executemany()` vs. one flattened multi-row `INSERT ... VALUES (...), (...), ...` statement, all inserting the same 500 rows with bound parameters: all three produced the *same* `find_spec` call count (~12,000+). Isolated further: 500 individual *non-parameterized* (literal SQL, no `?`) `execute()` calls produced **zero** `find_spec` calls. This proves the overhead scales with the number of *bound parameter values*, not the number of Python-level `execute()`/`executemany()` calls or distinct SQL statements — batching calls together doesn't reduce the total parameter count, so it doesn't reduce the cost.
  - [x] 9.2. **Root cause narrowed further than §11's write-up, via direct tracing of `importlib.machinery.PathFinder.find_spec`, not just counting calls**: every parameter-bound `conn.execute()` probes for `pandas` (confirmed by name, not just "plausibly pandas/pyarrow/numpy" as §11 guessed) via `importlib.find_spec`. This project doesn't install `pandas`/`pyarrow`/`polars`, and **Python never caches a *failed* import the way it caches a successful one in `sys.modules`** — every call re-walks the entire `sys.path` from scratch to re-confirm absence. Profiled a real scan of this project's own `src/drspec/db/` (91 functions): 556,612 `find_spec` calls alone.
  - [x] 9.3. Two fix candidates investigated and rejected before settling on the one shipped, each with verified reasoning, not assumed:
    1. A custom `sys.meta_path` finder caching confirmed-absent names and returning `None` (defer) on repeat lookups — doesn't help, since returning `None` just means "ask the next finder," which still does the full expensive walk.
    2. The same finder, but *raising* `ModuleNotFoundError` for a cached-absent name to actually short-circuit later finders — this does eliminate the overhead, but breaks `importlib.util.find_spec()`'s documented contract of returning `None` (never raising) for a missing module, which could be depended on elsewhere in the process.
  - [x] 9.4. **Fix shipped**: `src/drspec/db/connection.py` now sets `sys.modules[name] = None` for `pandas`/`pyarrow`/`polars`, but only after confirming via `importlib.util.find_spec(name) is None` that each is genuinely absent right now — never forced. This is the standard, documented Python idiom for "deliberately unavailable," respected correctly by both `import x` (raises `ModuleNotFoundError`, same as before, just instantly) and `importlib.util.find_spec(x)` (still returns `None`, not an exception) — unlike 9.3's rejected alternatives. A package that's actually installed is completely unaffected, since the check only sets the sentinel when `find_spec` already came back empty.
  - [x] 9.5. **Verified end-to-end on a real scan, not just the synthetic benchmark**: a clean A/B comparison (same fresh `_drspec` DB, same real `drspec scan src/drspec/core`) with the fix reverted vs. applied — **47.8s → 19.4s, a 2.46x speedup** — confirms the fix holds up on the actual write path, not just the isolated `find_spec`-counting microbenchmark.
  - [x] 9.6. Tests: `TestOptionalIntegrationProbeFix` in `test_db_connection.py` — correctness only (the fix never marks an actually-installed package absent; a real artifact insert/fetch round-trip still works; a genuinely-absent package's `import` still raises `ModuleNotFoundError` and `find_spec` still returns `None`, not an exception). No timing assertion in the test suite itself — timing is environment-dependent and would be flaky; the verified 2.46x number above is from a manual, reproducible A/B run, not an automated test. Full suite green (1186 passed, same 1 pre-existing unrelated failure).
  - [x] 9.7. Does not attempt §11's "Open for the dev" second question (whether this affects other write-heavy paths like `contract save`) — the fix is applied once, centrally, in `connection.py` (runs for every DuckDB connection this process ever opens), so it already covers every write path, not just scanning; no further per-command investigation needed.

  - [x] 10.1. New `drspec scan --embed/--no-embed` flag, default `--no-embed`, mirroring the existing `--queue/--no-queue` boolean-pair style (§12's proposed shape). Threaded through `sync_scan_results(..., embed: bool)` into `_sync_nodes`/`_sync_edges`/`_sync_references` and their callee-refresh/caller-diff helpers — `embed_text()` is skipped entirely (not computed, not just unwritten) for every new/changed node, edge, and reference when off.
  - [x] 10.2. Resolves §12's staleness open question: **option 2 (null out on change without `--embed`)**, the user's own stated leaning, implemented as-is. `insert_artifact`'s `embedding` parameter now always means "the value to write," `None` included — there's no more "leave it untouched" case, since every real caller now makes an explicit embed-or-don't decision every time. Verified end-to-end: a node embedded under `--embed`, then changed under a plain scan, has its embedding become `NULL`, not a stale leftover vector.
  - [x] 10.3. **Real bug found and fixed while implementing 10.2, not a pre-planned task — and a correction to this project's own understanding of the Task 2/3-era DuckDB FK bug, not just a new instance of it.** The existing workaround's docstring (since Task 2) claimed "updating a plain column on the same row is fine" and that only the array-typed `embedding` column triggers DuckDB's spurious FK `ConstraintException`. That assumption was **never actually exercised** — every real call site through Task 9 always passed a real `embedding`, so the "plain update, no embedding touched" code path was dead in production. Once `--no-embed` became the default, that path became the *common* case and immediately failed: reproduced directly, a bare `UPDATE artifacts SET status = ...` with **no array column involved at all** still raises the same FK exception whenever the row has an incoming `queue` or `dependencies` reference. Fixed by applying the `_fk_references_detached` wrapper unconditionally to every `artifacts` UPDATE in `insert_artifact`, not just ones that touch `embedding` — caught by two of the *existing* 3.7/3.11 regression tests (`test_deep_scan_flag_actually_widens_the_diff_scope`, `test_plain_scan_leaves_seeded_fallback_edge_untouched`) failing with exit code 1 the moment `--no-embed` became the default, not by new test-writing.
  - [x] 10.4. Resolves §12's other open question ("does `--semantic` need to handle zero embeddings gracefully"): yes, and now does. New `any_artifact_has_embedding(conn)` query; `drspec search --semantic` returns a `"message"` field explaining *why* the result is empty (never run `--embed`) when that's actually the cause, distinguishing it from a genuine zero-match result.
  - [x] 10.5. Considered and dropped a performance optimization (skip the FK-detach dance when there's no existing embedding to clear) once 10.3's broader bug was found — it would have reintroduced the exact same latent bug for the plain-column case, just under a different condition. Correctness over the extra optimization; the detach overhead is still far smaller than the embedding compute it replaces.

Tests: two new CLI-integration tests in `test_scan_graph_sync.py` (`--no-embed` leaves a new node's embedding `NULL`; a changed node's embedding clears when rescanned without `--embed`), two new tests in `test_db_queries.py` (10.3's regression — a plain status update survives a `queue` reference with no embedding involved at all; a changed node's embedding is explicitly nulled, not left stale), one new test in `test_search_command.py` (the zero-embeddings hint message), plus updates to the two pre-existing tests that asserted embeddings exist after a *plain* scan (now pass `--embed` explicitly, consistent with the new default). Full suite green (1191 passed, same 1 pre-existing unrelated failure).
